<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every prescribed-order finite sequence of vectors in the Euclidean unit ball of ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup> has one signing for which every signed prefix has norm at most $`C\sqrt d`$, with <i>C</i> absolute and independent of the sequence length. Consequently, every indexed zero-sum family of unit-ball vectors admits an ordering with the same bound for its unsigned partial sums. This determines the Euclidean Steinitz constant up to absolute factors, $`S_2(d)=\Theta(\sqrt d)`$, and resolves the Euclidean Steinitz–Bergström conjecture.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-signing)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for all integers $d,N\ge1$ and every sequence $v_1,\ldots,v_N\in\mathbb R^d$ with $\lVert v_i\rVert_2\le1$, there are signs $\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_N\in\{-1,1\}$ for which $$\max_{0\le k\le N} \left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^k\varepsilon_i v_i\right\rVert_2\le C\sqrt d.$$ The same signs control all prefixes, and $C$ is independent of $d$, $N$ and the sequence.
  - Explanation: One common signing controls every prescribed-order prefix, uniformly in sequence length.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-steinitz)): There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for all integers $d,N\ge1$ and every indexed family $v_1,\ldots,v_N\in\mathbb R^d$ satisfying $\lVert v_i\rVert_2\le1$ and $\sum_{i=1}^N v_i=0$, some permutation $\pi\in\mathfrak S_N$ satisfies $$\max_{0\le k\le N}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^k v_{\pi(i)}\right\rVert_2 \le C\sqrt d.$$ The constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a> also suffices here.
  - Explanation: A zero-sum indexed family admits an ordering controlling every unsigned prefix at the square-root dimension scale.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-adaptive)): Let $K\subset\mathbb R^m$ be nonempty, bounded, open, convex and centrally symmetric. Suppose that $$\label{eq:all-diagonal-energy} \lambda_Q(K)\le\kappa^2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q \quad\text{for every positive definite diagonal }Q.$$ Let $j_1,\ldots,j_s$ be distinct elements of $\{1,\ldots,m\}$, and let $q_i:\mathbb R^{i-1}\to[-\delta,\delta]$ be arbitrary functions, for $1\le i\le s$. There exist signs $\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_s$ such that the recursion $$x^{(0)}=0,\qquad x^{(i)}=x^{(i-1)}+ \bigl(q_i(x^{(i-1)}_{j_1},\ldots,x^{(i-1)}_{j_{i-1}}) +\varepsilon_i\bigr)e_{j_i}$$ has $x^{(s)}\in K$. For $i=1$, the function $q_1$ has a one-point domain.
  - Explanation: Uniform diagonal energy bounds permit distinct coordinate steps with bounded shifts depending on earlier assigned coordinates.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-filtered)): There are positive absolute constants $A_0,B_0,H_0$ with the following property. Let $d\ge1$ and $n\ge0$ be integers and put $m=d+n$. For $1\le t\le n$, let $C_t$ be an invertible real $d\times d$ matrix and let $b_t\in\mathbb R^d$ satisfy $$C_tC_t^T+b_tb_t^T=I_d.$$ Define linear maps $R_t:\mathbb R^m\to\mathbb R^d$ by $$\label{eq:filter-recursion} R_0x=(x_1,\ldots,x_d),\qquad R_tx=C_tR_{t-1}x+b_tx_{d+t}\quad(1\le t\le n).$$ Then the bounded open convex centrally symmetric set $$\label{eq:filtered-body} \mathcal D= \left\{x\in\mathbb R^m: \begin{array}{ll} \|x_j\|<B_0 & (1\le j\le m),\\ \lVert R_tx\rVert_2<A_0\sqrt d & (0\le t\le n) \end{array}\right\}$$ satisfies $$\lambda_Q(\mathcal D)\le H_0\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q \quad\text{for every positive definite diagonal }Q.$$
  - Explanation: The coefficient body controlling every filtered state has a diagonal energy bound independent of the number of maps.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-slab-energy)): Let $D\subset\mathbb R^m$ be bounded, nonempty, open, convex and centrally symmetric. Let $D_*>0$, $\delta>0$, and let $m_1,\ldots,m_n$ be real rows of length $m$. Set $$K=(2D_*D)\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^n\{x:\|m_i x\|<\delta\}.$$ For every positive definite diagonal $Q$, $$\label{eq:slab-energy} \lambda_Q(K) \le D_*^{-2}\lambda_Q(D) +\pi^2\delta^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^n m_iQm_i^T.$$
  - Explanation: The energy cost of imposing predictor slabs is bounded by a weighted quadratic sum of the rows.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-brownian-rate)): Let $D\subset\mathbb R^m$ be bounded, nonempty, open and convex, and let $Q$ be positive definite and diagonal. If $W$ is Brownian motion with covariance rate $2Q$, started at $x\in D$, then $$\label{eq:brownian-rate} \lim_{T\to\infty}-\frac1T \log\mathbb P_x\{W(s)\in D\text{ for all }0\le s\le T\} =\lambda_Q(D).$$ In particular, the starting point may be $0$ when $D$ is centrally symmetric.
  - Explanation: The Brownian survival exponent equals the diagonal Dirichlet energy in the stated bounded convex domain.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-path-correlation)): Let $X:[0,T]\to\mathbb R^m$ be a centered Gaussian process with continuous paths. For $i=1,\ldots,r$, let $E_i$ be an event of the form $$E_i=\bigcap_{j=1}^{r_i} \{A_{ij}X(s)\in K_{ij}\text{ for all }s\in I_{ij}\},$$ where $A_{ij}$ is linear, $K_{ij}$ is a closed centrally symmetric convex set in its target space, and $I_{ij}\subset[0,T]$ is a closed interval. Then $$\mathbb P\Bigl(\bigcap_{i=1}^r E_i\Bigr) \ge \prod_{i=1}^r\mathbb P(E_i).$$
  - Explanation: Centered Gaussian path constraints in closed symmetric convex sets satisfy a product probability lower bound.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-density)): Let $K\subset\mathbb R^m$ be a nonempty bounded open convex set symmetric about zero. If $$\lambda_Q(K)\le\kappa^2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q \qquad\text{for every positive definite diagonal }Q,$$ then there is a nonnegative $f\in H^1(\mathbb R^m)$, vanishing almost everywhere outside $K$, such that $$\int f^2=1,\qquad \int\|\partial_i f\|^2\le\kappa^2 \quad(1\le i\le m).$$
  - Explanation: All positive diagonal energy bounds yield a single normalized nonnegative function with simultaneous coordinate derivative bounds.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-minkowski)): Let $D_0,D_1\subset\mathbb R^m$ be nonempty bounded open convex sets symmetric about zero, and let $Q$ be positive definite diagonal. For $0\le\alpha\le1$, $$\lambda_Q((1-\alpha)D_0+\alpha D_1) \le(1-\alpha)\lambda_Q(D_0)+\alpha\lambda_Q(D_1).$$
  - Explanation: Diagonal Dirichlet energy is convex under Minkowski averages of the stated domains.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-shift-section)): Let $K\subset\mathbb R^m$ be a nonempty bounded open convex set symmetric about zero and satisfying $\lambda_Q(K)\le\kappa^2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q$ for every positive definite diagonal $Q$. For every coordinate vector $e$, there is another domain $T_e(K)$ with the same properties such that, for all $y\in T_e(K)$ and $\|q\|\le\delta$, some $\varepsilon\in\{-1,1\}$ satisfies $$y+(q+\varepsilon)e\in K.$$
  - Explanation: A transformed domain preserves the diagonal energy bounds and permits a signed step after any allowed shift.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-increment)): There are absolute constants $e_0>0$ and $C<\infty$ with the following property. For every recursion in Proposition <a href="#prop:filtered" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:filtered">4</a>, every positive definite diagonal $Q$, every dyadic $\theta$, and $0\le s\le t\le n$, if $E=E_\theta(s,t)\le e_0$, then $$\bigl\lVert(F_t^\theta-F_s^\theta) (I_m+Q/\theta)^{1/2}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le C\bigl(1+k_t(\theta)\bigr)E^{1/a}, \qquad a=\tfrac32. \label{eq:increment}$$
  - Explanation: Small additive spectral energy controls the weighted Hilbert–Schmidt variation of the localized filters.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-comparison)): There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for all $x,y>0$, $$\begin{split} \mathcal J(x,y)&\ge c(1+y)\|f_0(x)-f_0(y)\|^2,\\ \frac yx\mathcal J(y,x)&\ge c(1+y)\|f_0(x)-f_0(y)\|^2. \end{split} \label{eq:scalar-comparison}$$
  - Explanation: Scalar potential remainders dominate the squared differences of the cutoff values in either argument order.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoint-comparison)): With the preceding notation, $$\bigl\lVert(F_t-F_s)(I_m+Q/\theta)^{1/2}\bigr\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 \le C\left\{ \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(p(Y)-p(Z)\bigr) +\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(p(X)-p(Z)\bigr) \right\}. \label{eq:endpoint-comparison}$$
  - Explanation: Trace potential differences bound the weighted distance of the endpoint filters, including newly read coordinates.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-direct-gain)): For the matrices $X,Y,Z,V$ above, suppose $E=E_\theta(s,t)\le e_0$, where $$c_a=a2^a,\qquad e_0=\frac1{16c_a},\qquad a=\tfrac32.$$ If $q=(c_aE)^{1/a}$, then $q<1/4$ and $$V\preceq q(I_d+X), \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\bigl(p(X)-p(Z)\bigr)\le Cq\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\phi(X). \label{eq:direct-gain}$$
  - Explanation: The accumulated gain is bounded relative to the final matrix and its trace potential cost is controlled by spectral size.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ou-energy)): Let $D\subset\mathbb R^m$ be bounded, nonempty, open and convex. Define $$\lambda_Q^{\mathrm{OU}}(D) =\inf_{0\ne h\in H_0^1(D)} \frac{\int_D\langle Q\nabla h,\nabla h\rangle\,d\gamma_m} {\int_D h^2\,d\gamma_m}.$$ For every $T>0$, $$\label{eq:ou-comparison} \mathbb P\{U([0,T])\subset D\} \le e^{-T\lambda_Q^{\mathrm{OU}}(D)}, \qquad \lambda_Q(D)\le\lambda_Q^{\mathrm{OU}}(D)+\tfrac12\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q.$$
  - Explanation: Stationary Ornstein–Uhlenbeck survival bounds its Dirichlet energy, which compares to the Lebesgue Dirichlet energy.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-epoch-prob)): There is an absolute $c_1$ with the following property. Let $\mathcal I\subset\{0,\ldots,n\}$ be an index set whose values $h_t$ lie in an interval of length at most $e_0$, where $e_0$ is the constant in Lemma <a href="#lem:increment" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:increment">11</a>. For every $u\ge0$, $$\label{eq:epoch-prob} \mathbb P\left\{ \lVert F_tU(\tau)\rVert\le c_1\sqrt{k_t} \text{ for every }t\in\mathcal I, \ u\le\tau\le u+\theta^{-1} \right\}\ge\tfrac12.$$ The same conclusion holds on any shorter time interval.
  - Explanation: All filters in a bounded-energy index interval remain controlled over a matched time interval with uniform positive probability.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-lp-steinitz)): Let $1\le p\le\infty$, with $1/\infty=0$, and let $d,N\ge1$ be integers. Every sequence $v_1,\ldots,v_N\in\mathbb R^d$ with $\lVert v_i\rVert_p\le1$ admits signs $\varepsilon_i\in\{-1,1\}$ such that $$\max_{0\le k\le N} \left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^k\varepsilon_i v_i\right\rVert_p \le C d^{\max\{1/p,\,1-1/p\}}.$$ If additionally $\sum_{i=1}^N v_i=0$, there is a permutation $\pi\in\mathfrak S_N$ such that $$\max_{0\le k\le N} \left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^k v_{\pi(i)}\right\rVert_p \le C d^{\max\{1/p,\,1-1/p\}}.$$ Here $C$ is the same absolute constant as in Theorems <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a> and <a href="#thm:steinitz" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:steinitz">2</a>, independent of $p,d,N$. For $1\le p\le2$, both bounds have sharp dimension order $d^{1/p}$: for every $d$ there is a unit-ball sequence for which every signing has a prefix of norm at least $d^{1/p}$, and a finite indexed zero-sum unit-ball family for which every permutation has a prefix of norm at least $\tfrac13d^{1/p}$.
  - Explanation: Norm comparison extends the prefix signing and zero-sum ordering bounds to finite-dimensional norms, with sharp order in the stated range.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-colorful-steinitz)): Let $d,k,n\ge1$ be integers and let $a_i^j\in\mathbb R^d$, for $1\le j\le k$ and $1\le i\le n$, satisfy $$\lVert a_i^j\rVert_2\le1, \qquad \sum_{j=1}^k\sum_{i=1}^n a_i^j=0.$$ There is one tuple of permutations $(\pi_1,\ldots,\pi_k)\in\mathfrak S_n^k$ such that $$\max_{0\le m\le n} \left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^k\sum_{i=1}^m a_{\pi_j(i)}^j\right\rVert_2 \le 2C\sqrt d,$$ where $C$ is the absolute constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a>. The same tuple works for every $m$ and may be chosen jointly from the whole array. Only the global total sum is assumed to vanish; the bound controls the displayed aggregate synchronous prefixes, not each row separately.
  - Explanation: Joint row permutations control synchronous aggregate prefixes under a global zero-sum condition; separate row bounds are not asserted.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Euclidean Steinitz constant** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Let $S_2(d)$ be the supremum of $\beta$ over all finite indexed zero-sum families of Euclidean unit-ball vectors in $\mathbb R^d$.
- **Rearrangement prefix radius** ([source](#pm-h-2)): For an indexed family with $\sum_{i=1}^N v_i=0$, define $$\beta(v_1,\ldots,v_N) =\min_{\pi\in\mathfrak S_N}\; \max_{0\le k\le N}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^k v_{\pi(i)}\right\rVert_2,$$ where $\mathfrak S_N$ is the set of permutations of $\{1,\ldots,N\}$ and an empty sum is zero.
- **Diagonal Dirichlet energy** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Define its diagonal Dirichlet energy by $$\label{eq:dirichlet-energy} \lambda_Q(D)= \inf_{0\ne g\in H^1_0(D)} \frac{\displaystyle\int_D\langle Q\nabla g,\nabla g\rangle\,dx} {\displaystyle\int_Dg^2\,dx}.$$
- **Central symmetry** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Central symmetry will always mean symmetry about the origin.
- **Matrix order** ([source](#pm-h-5)): For symmetric matrices, $A\le B$ means that $B-A$ is positive semidefinite.
- **Clipped predictor** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For $u\in\mathbb R$ write $\operatorname{clip}_\delta(u)=\max\{-\delta,\min\{u,\delta\}\}$.
- **Hilbert–Schmidt norm** ([source](#sec:matrix)): For a real matrix $A$, write $\lVert A\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(AA^\top)$.
- **Additive scale energy** ([source](#eq:energy-definition)): Define $$E_\theta(s,t)=\sum_{i=s+1}^t \left\{ \mathop{\mathrm{tr}}h(X_{i-1})-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}h(\widehat X_i) +\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}h(X_i)-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}h(\widehat X_i) \right\}, \qquad 0\le s\le t\le n. \label{eq:energy-definition}$$
- **Ornstein–Uhlenbeck Dirichlet energy** ([source](#stmt-lem-ou-energy)): Define $$\lambda_Q^{\mathrm{OU}}(D) =\inf_{0\ne h\in H_0^1(D)} \frac{\int_D\langle Q\nabla h,\nabla h\rangle\,d\gamma_m} {\int_D h^2\,d\gamma_m}.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-signing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slab-energy): Predictor cost and exact feedback cancellation -> One common signing controls every prescribed-order prefix, uniformly in sequence length.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-thm-steinitz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-steinitz): Positive-forward, negative-reverse transference -> A zero-sum indexed family admits an ordering controlling every unsigned prefix at the square-root dimension scale.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-adaptive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shift-section-2): Backward domain transformations and forward shifted signs -> Uniform diagonal energy bounds permit distinct coordinate steps with bounded shifts depending on earlier assigned coordinates.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-filtered) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-epoch-prob-2): Gaussian correlation across spectral scales and time intervals -> The coefficient body controlling every filtered state has a diagonal energy bound independent of the number of maps.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-slab-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-path-correlation-2): Brownian survival under symmetric slab intersections -> The energy cost of imposing predictor slabs is bounded by a weighted quadratic sum of the rows.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-brownian-rate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-brownian-rate): Killed semigroup spectral decay -> The Brownian survival exponent equals the diagonal Dirichlet energy in the stated bounded convex domain.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-gaussian-path-correlation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gaussian-path-correlation): Finite Gaussian grids and decreasing path-event limits -> Centered Gaussian path constraints in closed symmetric convex sets satisfy a product probability lower bound.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-lem-simultaneous-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-simultaneous-density): Convex derivative-energy upper sets and strict separation -> All positive diagonal energy bounds yield a single normalized nonnegative function with simultaneous coordinate derivative bounds.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-minkowski) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-minkowski): Gaussian log-concavity and Brownian scaling -> Diagonal Dirichlet energy is convex under Minkowski averages of the stated domains.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-shift-section) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shift-section): Lifted Steiner symmetrization and Jensen at the mean height -> A transformed domain preserves the diagonal energy bounds and permits a signed step after any allowed shift.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-lem-increment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-direct-gain-2): Endpoint comparison and accumulated gain control -> Small additive spectral energy controls the weighted Hilbert–Schmidt variation of the localized filters.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-comparison): Scalar potential remainder and cutoff Lipschitz comparison -> Scalar potential remainders dominate the squared differences of the cutoff values in either argument order.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-lem-endpoint-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoint-comparison): Old-coordinate and new-coordinate support expansions -> Trace potential differences bound the weighted distance of the endpoint filters, including newly read coordinates.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-lem-direct-gain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-direct-gain): Relative gain potential stable under contractions -> The accumulated gain is bounded relative to the final matrix and its trace potential cost is controlled by spectral size.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-lem-ou-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ou-energy): Killed Ornstein–Uhlenbeck semigroup and Gaussian-density conjugation -> Stationary Ornstein–Uhlenbeck survival bounds its Dirichlet energy, which compares to the Lebesgue Dirichlet energy.
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-lem-epoch-prob) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-epoch-prob): Energy-coordinate chaining and Gaussian enlargement -> All filters in a bounded-energy index interval remain controlled over a matched time interval with uniform positive probability.
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-cor-lp-steinitz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-lp-steinitz): Norm comparisons and explicit lower-bound families -> Norm comparison extends the prefix signing and zero-sum ordering bounds to finite-dimensional norms, with sharp order in the stated range.
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-cor-colorful-steinitz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-colorful-steinitz): Column-major signing and matrix transference -> Joint row permutations control synchronous aggregate prefixes under a global zero-sum condition; separate row bounds are not asserted.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **signed-prefixes**: A single signing controls every prescribed-order signed prefix by a constant bound.
- **zero-sum-ordering**: An indexed zero-sum family admits a permutation controlling every unsigned prefix by a constant bound.
- **index-preservation**: Reordering preserves multiplicities and permits repetitions and zero vectors.
- **offline-existence**: The sign choices may depend on the complete input sequence and supply neither an online rule nor an efficient algorithm.
- **colorful-aggregate**: A single jointly chosen tuple of row permutations bounds aggregate synchronous prefixes by a larger constant bound.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **online-signing**: Choose each sign irrevocably using only the observed prefix. -> The existence proof may use the complete sequence.
- **efficient-implementation**: Deploy the theorem as an efficient signing or ordering algorithm. -> Efficient computation is not established.
- **approximate-balance**: Apply the unsigned ordering bound to a batch whose total is only approximately zero. -> The ordering hypothesis is not met.
- **unsigned-fixed-order**: Keep all contributions unsigned in a prescribed order and assume the signing bound. -> The allowed sign choice has been removed.
- **constant-infinity-prefix**: Infer a constant infinity-norm bound for all prefixes. -> The Euclidean all-prefix conclusion does not establish that bound.
- **separate-prefix-signings**: Apply a terminal balancing result separately to prefixes and claim a common signing. -> The selected signs may change between prefixes.
- **individual-row-bounds**: Use the colorful result to bound each row separately. -> The controlled prefixes aggregate contributions across rows.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The construction is existential: its choices may depend on the complete input sequence. Its successive choice of signs does not supply an online rule or an efficient algorithm. The row permutations are existential, with no online or efficient algorithm claimed. The finite transference argument assumes the existential signing; it does not provide a complete constructive signing procedure.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | A finite sequence of vector-valued contributions can model accumulated error when the application permits choosing signs. The result gives an existence guarantee for every prefix under Euclidean unit-ball hypotheses; a software construction is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A game or simulation contract is ABSENT. Brownian and Gaussian processes are used to prove analytic bounds, without a simulation method or fidelity guarantee. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A tool implementation, interface or operational contract is ABSENT. The paper proves existence of signs and permutations without an efficient construction. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The finite transference gives a precise mathematical transformation of supplied signs into an ordering. The proof also assumes an order minimizing the largest prefix norm, so it does not provide an efficient procedure for obtaining the required inputs. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A data-structure design or complexity result is ABSENT. Indexed families distinguish repeated vectors, but the paper does not prescribe a representation or update interface. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An accelerator implementation or hardware model is ABSENT. The matrix contractions and spectral functions are analytic proof objects without a hardware execution contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A cryptographic or security contract is ABSENT. The existence of signs with small Euclidean prefixes does not supply a security definition or an adversarial guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A learning objective, training method or generalization guarantee is ABSENT. Vector balancing alone does not establish a connection between prefix norms and learning performance. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | inspirational | inferred | The zero-sum ordering theorem provides a mathematical existence bound for minimizing the largest Euclidean running total over permutations. It can frame a finite sequencing objective when the exact vector hypotheses hold; an optimization algorithm is ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A distributed protocol, communication model or consistency contract is ABSENT. The paper permits choices depending on the whole input and does not establish a distributed method for making them. |
| other (Vector balancing and convex analysis) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The paper directly studies finite vector balancing, zero-sum rearrangement and diagonal Dirichlet energy on convex domains. These are mathematical existence and analytic bounds rather than an efficient engineering construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The prescribed-order guarantee requires Euclidean unit-ball vectors and freedom to choose signs; it does not bound fixed-order unsigned sums. Blocked use: ['Unsigned accumulation when signs cannot be changed.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The unsigned ordering guarantee assumes an exactly zero total and permits a permutation of indexed occurrences. Blocked use: ['Approximately zero-sum batches without an additional argument.', 'Ordering under additional precedence constraints not justified by the paper.']
- **L3** (nonconstructive): The construction is existential and can depend on the complete sequence; no online rule or efficient algorithm is supplied. Blocked use: ['Online sign assignment.', 'Runtime, space or deployability guarantees attributed to this construction.']
- **L4** (edge_case): Repeated vectors and zero vectors are allowed; the empty-sequence and dimension-zero extensions have zero partial sums. Blocked use: ['Discarding indexed multiplicities.', 'Claiming that the positive-dimension theorem excludes the stated trivial extensions.']
- **L5** (counterexample_scope): Terminal balancing and separate prefix signings do not establish one common all-prefix signing; the present Euclidean estimate does not assert a constant infinity-norm all-prefix bound. Blocked use: ['Combining unrelated signings chosen separately for each prefix.', 'A constant infinity-norm all-prefix guarantee.']
- **L6** (counterexample_scope): The regular-simplex example forces square-root dimension growth regardless of ordering. Blocked use: ['A dimension-independent uniform Euclidean ordering bound.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The adaptive-shift proposition requires the energy bound for every positive definite diagonal weight; checking a single weight does not establish the premise. Blocked use: ['Replacing the all-diagonal spectral hypothesis by a single-weight test.']
- **L8** (nonconstructive): Adaptive shifts do not make the sign selection online: the choice can depend on the whole body and all prescribed functions. Blocked use: ['Treating the forward recursion as an efficient causal sign-selection procedure.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): The filtered-body energy theorem requires invertible filters and the stated covariance identity; arbitrary filters are outside its premise. Blocked use: ['Singular or unrelated filter recursions without a new proof.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The matrix increment estimate applies only within its small-energy regime. Blocked use: ['Using the stated increment estimate on an interval whose energy exceeds the threshold.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): Gaussian path correlation requires a centered continuous Gaussian process and closed centrally symmetric convex path constraints. Blocked use: ['Replacing the process or constraint geometry while retaining the correlation guarantee.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Scope note of family 097 (`lean/docs/097.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):

>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result gives the Euclidean Steinitz–Bergström bound with one absolute constant $C$. For every finite family of vectors in the unit ball of $\mathbb R^d$, signs can be chosen so that every prefix in the prescribed order has norm at most $C\sqrt d$. When the vector sum is zero, a permutation makes every unsigned prefix satisfy the same bound. Repeated and zero vectors are included. The result is existential and does not supply an online algorithm.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `Vector` | abbrev |
| `SignedPrefixBound` | def |
| `OrderingPrefixBound` | def |
| `main` | theorem |

Comparator source `SteinitzBergstrom.lean` (965 bytes, sha256 `c36334eff43ee1dc46d565c80a4a537fea67951e727ab7b88b5c64fca0dfe63d`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace EuclideanSteinitzBergstrom

abbrev Vector (d : ℕ) := EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin d)

def SignedPrefixBound (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (d N : ℕ), 1 ≤ d → 1 ≤ N →
    ∀ (v : Fin N → Vector d), (∀ i, ‖v i‖ ≤ 1) →
      ∃ ε : Fin N → ℝ, (∀ i, ε i = -1 ∨ ε i = 1) ∧
        ∀ k : ℕ, k ≤ N →
          ‖∑ i ∈ Finset.univ.filter (fun i : Fin N => i.val < k), ε i • v i‖ ≤
            C * Real.sqrt d

def OrderingPrefixBound (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (d N : ℕ), 1 ≤ d → 1 ≤ N →
    ∀ (v : Fin N → Vector d), (∀ i, ‖v i‖ ≤ 1) → (∑ i, v i) = 0 →
      ∃ π : Equiv.Perm (Fin N), ∀ k : ℕ, k ≤ N →
        ‖∑ i ∈ Finset.univ.filter (fun i : Fin N => i.val < k), v (π i)‖ ≤
          C * Real.sqrt d

theorem main : ∃ C : ℝ, SignedPrefixBound C ∧ OrderingPrefixBound C := by
  sorry

end EuclideanSteinitzBergstrom

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every prescribed-order finite sequence of vectors in the Euclidean unit ball of ℝ<sup><i>d</i></sup> has one signing for which every signed prefix has norm at most $`C\sqrt d`$, with <i>C</i> absolute and independent of the sequence length. Consequently, every indexed zero-sum family of unit-ball vectors admits an ordering with the same bound for its unsigned partial sums. This determines the Euclidean Steinitz constant up to absolute factors, $`S_2(d)=\Theta(\sqrt d)`$, and resolves the Euclidean Steinitz–Bergström conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

Let $v_1,\ldots,v_N$ be vectors in the Euclidean unit ball of $\mathbb R^d$. There are two natural ways to keep their partial sums small: choose signs in a prescribed order, or, when the family sums to zero, choose an order without changing any signs. The difficulty in the first problem is to control every prefix by one choice of signs, with a bound independent of $N$. We prove such a bound on the square-root scale in the dimension.

<a id="stmt-thm-signing"></a>
<div id="thm:signing" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Prescribed-order signed prefixes). *There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for all integers $d,N\ge1$ and every sequence $v_1,\ldots,v_N\in\mathbb R^d$ with $\lVert v_i\rVert_2\le1$, there are signs $\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_N\in\{-1,1\}$ for which $$\max_{0\le k\le N}
 \left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^k\varepsilon_i v_i\right\rVert_2\le C\sqrt d.$$ The same signs control all prefixes, and $C$ is independent of $d$, $N$ and the sequence.*

</div>

The zero-sum ordering problem is quantified by the Euclidean Steinitz constant. For an indexed family with $\sum_{i=1}^N v_i=0$, define $$\beta(v_1,\ldots,v_N)
 =\min_{\pi\in\mathfrak S_N}\;
   \max_{0\le k\le N}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^k v_{\pi(i)}\right\rVert_2,$$ where $\mathfrak S_N$ is the set of permutations of $\{1,\ldots,N\}$ and an empty sum is zero. Let $S_2(d)$ be the supremum of $\beta$ over all finite indexed zero-sum families of Euclidean unit-ball vectors in $\mathbb R^d$. The Euclidean Steinitz–Bergström conjecture asks whether $S_2(d)=O(\sqrt d)$. Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a> gives its affirmative resolution.

<a id="stmt-thm-steinitz"></a>
<div id="thm:steinitz" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Euclidean Steinitz–Bergström bound). *There is an absolute constant $C$ such that, for all integers $d,N\ge1$ and every indexed family $v_1,\ldots,v_N\in\mathbb R^d$ satisfying $\lVert v_i\rVert_2\le1$ and $\sum_{i=1}^N v_i=0$, some permutation $\pi\in\mathfrak S_N$ satisfies $$\max_{0\le k\le N}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^k v_{\pi(i)}\right\rVert_2
 \le C\sqrt d.$$ The constant in Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a> also suffices here.*

</div>

Both statements allow repetitions and zero vectors. The empty-sequence and dimension-zero extensions have every partial sum equal to zero. The construction is existential: its choices may depend on the complete input sequence. Its successive choice of signs does not supply an online rule or an efficient algorithm.

The order of growth in Theorem <a href="#thm:steinitz" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:steinitz">2</a> is necessary. In the $d$-dimensional hyperplane orthogonal to the all-ones vector $\mathbf1\in\mathbb R^{d+1}$, let $e_i$ denote the standard coordinate vectors and take $$u_i=\sqrt{\frac{d+1}{d}}\left(e_i-\frac{\mathbf1}{d+1}\right),
 \qquad 1\le i\le d+1.$$ These unit vectors sum to zero and satisfy $\langle u_i,u_j\rangle=-1/d$ for $i\ne j$. Hence every sum of $k$ distinct members has squared norm $k(d+1-k)/d$. With $k=\lfloor(d+1)/2\rfloor$, this is at least $d/4$, regardless of the ordering. Thus $$\tfrac12\sqrt d\le S_2(d)\le C\sqrt d\qquad(d\ge1).$$

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### From signs to an ordering

We give the short transference argument at once. Chobanyan’s transference principle (Sergej Chobanyan 1994) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Chobanyan1994](#ref-Chobanyan1994)] relates rearrangements and signed sums. We use its finite positive-forward, negative-reverse form (Sergei Chobanyan et al. 2023, Theorem 2.1 and Remark 1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ChobanyanEtAl2023](#ref-ChobanyanEtAl2023)].

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-steinitz"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-steinitz</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:steinitz" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:steinitz">2</a> from Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a>.* Fix an indexed zero-sum family and choose an order attaining its minimum $\beta$; this exists because there are only finitely many permutations. Relabel the vectors in that order. Put $$A_j=\sum_{i=1}^jv_i,
 \qquad B_j=\sum_{i=1}^j\varepsilon_i v_i,
 \qquad 0\le j\le N,$$ where the signs come from Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a>. Hence $\lVert A_j\rVert_2\le\beta$ and $\lVert B_j\rVert_2\le C\sqrt d$. The positive-sign and negative-sign selections from an original prefix sum respectively to $$P_j=\frac{A_j+B_j}{2},\qquad M_j=\frac{A_j-B_j}{2}.$$ Place all positive-sign indices in their original order, followed by all negative-sign indices in reverse original order. A partial sum in the first block is some $P_j$. In the second block, the unused indices are the negative-sign indices of an original prefix. Since the total sum is zero, the current partial sum is therefore some $-M_j$. This also covers either block being empty. Every new partial sum has norm at most $(\beta+C\sqrt d)/2$. By minimality, $$\beta\le\frac{\beta+C\sqrt d}{2},
 \qquad\text{and hence}\qquad \beta\le C\sqrt d.$$ This permutes indices and so preserves all multiplicities. ◻

</details>

The signed-prefix bound also yields finite-dimensional $\ell_p$ estimates and a colorful row-permutation theorem. We develop these distinct consequences in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">6</a>.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Historical context

The finite rearrangement theorem goes back to Steinitz’s work on conditionally convergent vector series; Ambrus and Heck give a historical account in (Ambrus and Heck 2026, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[AmbrusHeck2026](#ref-AmbrusHeck2026)]. The dimension bound for zero-sum rearrangement holds for arbitrary norms: Grinberg and Sevast’yanov (Grinberg and Sevast’yanov 1980, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GrinbergSevastyanov1980](#ref-GrinbergSevastyanov1980)] prove that the partial sums can be kept within $d$ times the unit ball. The smaller square-root scale in Euclidean space has a long history; Behrend discusses this expected growth in (Behrend 1954, 108) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Behrend1954](#ref-Behrend1954)]. The modern formulation and its attribution to Bergström are recorded by Ambrus and Heck (Ambrus and Heck 2026, Conjecture 5) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[AmbrusHeck2026](#ref-AmbrusHeck2026)]. Their Theorem 7 reduces the asymptotic question to a relaxed rearrangement problem for vectors with norms in $[1-\varepsilon,1]$, with $0<\varepsilon\le1$ fixed independently of the dimension. In that problem each prefix is compared with its proportional share of the total sum, rather than with zero. For prescribed-order Euclidean signing, the square-root-dimension question is stated explicitly by Bansal, Jiang, Meka, Singla and Sinha (Bansal et al. 2021, sec. 6, Conjecture 6.3) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BansalEtAl2021Full](#ref-BansalEtAl2021Full)], following Banaszczyk (Banaszczyk 2012) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Banaszczyk2012](#ref-Banaszczyk2012)]. Their discussion records Banaszczyk’s bound $O(\sqrt d+\sqrt{\log N})$; the length term becomes significant when $N$ is large compared with the dimension. In a 2026 preprint, Dutta, Jha and Jiang state an efficient prescribed-order signing bound $$O\bigl(\sqrt d+d^{1/4}\log^{7/4}N\bigr),$$ and the corresponding zero-sum ordering bound (Dutta, Jha, and Jiang 2026, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DuttaJhaJiang2026](#ref-DuttaJhaJiang2026)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a> gives an absolute multiple of $\sqrt d$ for every finite length.

Terminal vector balancing has a different quantifier. For columns in the Euclidean unit ball, Li (Li 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Li2026](#ref-Li2026)] bounds the final signed sum in infinity norm by an absolute constant and gives a deterministic polynomial-bit algorithm on rational input. That terminal statement implies a Euclidean bound of order $\sqrt d$ for one sum. Applying it separately to each prefix permits the signs to change with the prefix; Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a> instead supplies one common signing. The present all-prefix estimate is Euclidean and does not assert a constant infinity-norm all-prefix bound.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy

Gaussian convex bodies are a central setting for vector balancing, notably in the theorem of Banaszczyk (Banaszczyk 1998) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Banaszczyk1998](#ref-Banaszczyk1998)]. Our proof takes place in a space of coefficients: one coordinate is reserved for each incoming vector, together with $d$ initial state coordinates. A sequence of linear maps sends these coefficients to states in $\mathbb R^d$. At each step an invertible contraction acts on the old state and the next coefficient injects a multiple of the incoming vector. The body of coefficients for which every state is bounded will provide the simultaneous control of all prefixes.

Two analytic ingredients make this construction possible. First, if a bounded symmetric convex body has small Dirichlet energy for every positive diagonal weight, it supports a single square root of a probability density with small derivatives in every coordinate. A lifted Steiner symmetrization preserves these bounds and permits a coordinate step of the form $q+\varepsilon$, where $|q|\le1/4$ and $\varepsilon\in\{-1,1\}$. This adapts the directional-density and symmetrization method of Guo, Fang and Lu (Guo, Fang, and Lu 2026, secs. 3–4) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[GuoFangLu2026](#ref-GuoFangLu2026)].[^1] Bandeira (Bandeira 2026) gives a related formulation for terminal balancing in terms of Dirichlet eigenvalues. Akbas and Sra (Akbas and Sra 2026, Lemmas 2.2–2.3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[AkbasSra2026](#ref-AkbasSra2026)] develop the common quadratic-energy witness and lifted $L^2$-symmetrization argument. Here the hypotheses use diagonal weights, and the coordinate shifts may depend on earlier choices. We prove the required energy and shifted-step statements in full.

Second, the filtered body has a diagonal Dirichlet-energy bound independent of the number of filters. To prove this, we follow a stationary Ornstein–Uhlenbeck process in coefficient space and localize its filtered states to dyadic spectral scales. A matrix estimate controls changes at one scale by an additive trace budget, including gains inserted between noncommuting contractions. At a fixed scale, this estimate gives a uniform positive probability for all constraints in a group of indices with bounded accumulated energy, over a matched time interval. Gaussian correlation combines these groups, scales and time intervals; the exponential survival rate yields the energy bound.

The two ingredients meet through a scalar predictor. The allowed shift in the incoming coefficient exactly compensates for the contraction of the old state. Consequently the final states are the signed partial sums themselves. We give this complete construction in Section <a href="#sec:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:construction">2</a>, after stating the two analytic propositions. Section <a href="#sec:shifted-signs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:shifted-signs">3</a> proves the shifted-sign proposition. Section <a href="#sec:matrix" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:matrix">4</a> establishes the matrix variation estimate, and Section <a href="#sec:survival" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:survival">5</a> uses it to prove the filtered-body energy bound. Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">6</a> develops the norm-comparison and colorful row-permutation consequences.

Throughout, all unspecified constants are absolute. Vectors and matrices are real; $\lVert\cdot\rVert_\mathrm{op}$ and $\lVert\cdot\rVert_\mathrm{HS}$ denote operator and Hilbert–Schmidt norms. For symmetric matrices, $A\le B$ means that $B-A$ is positive semidefinite. Functions of positive definite matrices are defined by the spectral calculus.

<a id="sec:construction"></a>
### Constructing the signed walk

We will encode the signed walk by one point in a convex body in a larger coefficient space. The body controls a contracted version of every prefix. Its extra coordinates will be chosen so that their contributions exactly restore the parts removed by the contractions. This section states the two analytic inputs and proves Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a> from them.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The two analytic inputs

Let $D\subset\mathbb R^m$ be a nonempty bounded open convex set, and let $Q$ be a positive definite diagonal $m\times m$ matrix. Define its diagonal Dirichlet energy by <a id="eq:dirichlet-energy"></a>$$\label{eq:dirichlet-energy}
 \lambda_Q(D)=
 \inf_{0\ne g\in H^1_0(D)}
 \frac{\displaystyle\int_D\langle Q\nabla g,\nabla g\rangle\,dx}
      {\displaystyle\int_Dg^2\,dx}.$$ Here $H^1_0(D)$ is the closure of $C_c^\infty(D)$ in the Sobolev norm, and derivatives are weak derivatives. This normalization corresponds to Brownian motion with covariance rate $2Q$. Central symmetry will always mean symmetry about the origin. Fix <a id="eq:signing-constants"></a>$$\label{eq:signing-constants}
 \kappa=\frac1{100},\qquad \delta=\frac14.$$

Our first input turns small coordinate energies into signs while allowing a bounded shift at each step. The shifts may depend on previously assigned coordinates.

<a id="stmt-prop-adaptive"></a>
<div id="prop:adaptive" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:all-diagonal-energy"></a>
**Proposition 3** (Signs with adaptive shifts). *Let $K\subset\mathbb R^m$ be nonempty, bounded, open, convex and centrally symmetric. Suppose that $$\label{eq:all-diagonal-energy}
 \lambda_Q(K)\le\kappa^2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q
 \quad\text{for every positive definite diagonal }Q.$$ Let $j_1,\ldots,j_s$ be distinct elements of $\{1,\ldots,m\}$, and let $q_i:\mathbb R^{i-1}\to[-\delta,\delta]$ be arbitrary functions, for $1\le i\le s$. There exist signs $\varepsilon_1,\ldots,\varepsilon_s$ such that the recursion $$x^{(0)}=0,\qquad
 x^{(i)}=x^{(i-1)}+
 \bigl(q_i(x^{(i-1)}_{j_1},\ldots,x^{(i-1)}_{j_{i-1}})
       +\varepsilon_i\bigr)e_{j_i}$$ has $x^{(s)}\in K$. For $i=1$, the function $q_1$ has a one-point domain.*

</div>

This is an existence statement; the choice of signs can depend on the whole body $K$ and all the prescribed functions. Its proof uses backward transformations of $K$, followed by forward choices of the signs.

The second input supplies a body satisfying a uniform energy bound. Its linear maps retain a $d$-dimensional state and read one new coordinate at each step.

<a id="stmt-prop-filtered"></a>
<div id="prop:filtered" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:filter-recursion"></a>
**Proposition 4** (Energy of a filtered path body). *There are positive absolute constants $A_0,B_0,H_0$ with the following property. Let $d\ge1$ and $n\ge0$ be integers and put $m=d+n$. For $1\le t\le n$, let $C_t$ be an invertible real $d\times d$ matrix and let $b_t\in\mathbb R^d$ satisfy $$C_tC_t^T+b_tb_t^T=I_d.$$ Define linear maps $R_t:\mathbb R^m\to\mathbb R^d$ by $$\label{eq:filter-recursion}
 R_0x=(x_1,\ldots,x_d),\qquad
 R_tx=C_tR_{t-1}x+b_tx_{d+t}\quad(1\le t\le n).$$ Then the bounded open convex centrally symmetric set <a id="eq:filtered-body"></a>$$\label{eq:filtered-body}
 \mathcal D=
 \left\{x\in\mathbb R^m:
 \begin{array}{ll}
 |x_j|<B_0 & (1\le j\le m),\\
 \lVert R_tx\rVert_2<A_0\sqrt d & (0\le t\le n)
 \end{array}\right\}$$ satisfies $$\lambda_Q(\mathcal D)\le H_0\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q
 \quad\text{for every positive definite diagonal }Q.$$*

</div>

The constants are independent of the number of maps and of $Q$. This uniformity is essential: the application will use $n$ equal to the number of vectors. To compensate for the contractions, we will also impose linear constraints on the coefficients. The following estimate bounds their additional energy cost; we prove it after completing the construction.

<a id="stmt-lem-slab-energy"></a>
<div id="lem:slab-energy" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:slab-energy"></a>
**Lemma 5** (Adding slabs). *Let $D\subset\mathbb R^m$ be bounded, nonempty, open, convex and centrally symmetric. Let $D_*>0$, $\delta>0$, and let $m_1,\ldots,m_n$ be real rows of length $m$. Set $$K=(2D_*D)\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^n\{x:|m_i x|<\delta\}.$$ For every positive definite diagonal $Q$, $$\label{eq:slab-energy}
 \lambda_Q(K)
 \le D_*^{-2}\lambda_Q(D)
     +\pi^2\delta^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^n m_iQm_i^T.$$*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The filter, the shifts, and their cancellation

We now prove the signed-prefix theorem from Propositions <a href="#prop:adaptive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:adaptive">3</a> and <a href="#prop:filtered" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:filtered">4</a>. Their proofs in the remaining sections are independent of this application.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-slab-energy"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-slab-energy</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a>.* Write the prescribed sequence as $v_1,\ldots,v_n\in\mathbb R^d$, with $\lVert v_i\rVert_2\le1$. The hypotheses give $d,n\ge1$.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### The symmetric contractions.

For a positive absolute $\sigma<1$, to be chosen below, set <a id="eq:symmetric-filter"></a>$$\label{eq:symmetric-filter}
 b_i=\sigma v_i,\qquad
 \eta_i=\frac1{1+\sqrt{1-\lVert b_i\rVert_2^2}},\qquad
 C_i=I-\eta_ib_ib_i^T.$$ On the span of $b_i$, the matrix $C_i$ has eigenvalue $\sqrt{1-\lVert b_i\rVert_2^2}$, and on its orthogonal complement it is the identity. This includes $b_i=0$, for which $\eta_i=1/2$ and $C_i=I$. Consequently $$C_i=(I-b_ib_i^T)^{1/2}=C_i^T\succ0,
 \qquad C_i^2+b_ib_i^T=I,
 \qquad \frac12\le\eta_i\le1.$$ Use these matrices in <a href="#eq:filter-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:filter-recursion">[eq:filter-recursion]</a>, and let $\mathcal D$ be the body in Proposition <a href="#prop:filtered" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:filtered">4</a>.

For $x\in\mathbb R^{d+n}$, let $x^0$ be obtained by setting its first $d$ coordinates to zero. Define row vectors $m_i$ by <a id="eq:predictor-rows"></a>$$\label{eq:predictor-rows}
 m_ix=\eta_i b_i^TR_{i-1}x^0.$$ Each row $m_i$ is supported only on the noise coordinates $d+1,\ldots,d+i-1$. In particular it is unaffected by any coordinate assigned at step $i$ or later.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
###### The cost of the predictor constraints.

<a id="eq:predictor-weighted-cost"></a>
Let $M$ be the matrix with rows $m_i$. We claim that every column of $M$ has squared Euclidean norm at most $\sigma^2$. Its initial $d$ columns are zero. For a noise coordinate $d+j$, follow its contribution to the state: $$r_j=b_j,\qquad r_i=C_ir_{i-1}\quad(j<i\le n).$$ The coefficient of that coordinate in $m_i$ is zero for $i\le j$, and is $\eta_i b_i^Tr_{i-1}$ for $i>j$. Symmetry of $C_i$ gives the exact loss identity $$\lVert r_{i-1}\rVert_2^2-\lVert r_i\rVert_2^2
 =(b_i^Tr_{i-1})^2.$$ Thus the losses telescope: <a id="eq:predictor-columns"></a>$$\label{eq:predictor-columns}
 \begin{aligned}
 \lVert Me_{d+j}\rVert_2^2
 &=\sum_{i=j+1}^n\eta_i^2(b_i^Tr_{i-1})^2\\
 &\le\sum_{i=j+1}^n
       \bigl(\lVert r_{i-1}\rVert_2^2-\lVert r_i\rVert_2^2\bigr)
 \le\lVert b_j\rVert_2^2\le\sigma^2.
 \end{aligned}$$ In particular, for every positive definite diagonal $Q$, $$\label{eq:predictor-weighted-cost}
 \sum_{i=1}^n m_iQm_i^T
 =\sum_{j=1}^{d+n}Q_{jj}\lVert Me_j\rVert_2^2
 \le\sigma^2\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q.$$

Choose an absolute dilation $D_*>0$ and define <a id="eq:application-body"></a>$$\label{eq:application-body}
 K=(2D_*\mathcal D)\cap
   \{x:|m_ix|<\delta\text{ for }1\le i\le n\}.$$ This is a bounded open convex centrally symmetric set containing a neighborhood of zero. Lemma <a href="#lem:slab-energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:slab-energy">5</a>, Proposition <a href="#prop:filtered" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:filtered">4</a>, and <a href="#eq:predictor-weighted-cost" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:predictor-weighted-cost">[eq:predictor-weighted-cost]</a> give <a id="eq:application-energy"></a>$$\label{eq:application-energy}
 \lambda_Q(K)
 \le\left(H_0D_*^{-2}+\pi^2\delta^{-2}\sigma^2\right)\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}Q.$$ We can therefore fix, once and for all, <a id="eq:absolute-parameters"></a>$$\label{eq:absolute-parameters}
 D_*\ge\max\{1,\sqrt{2H_0}/\kappa\},\qquad
 \sigma=\min\{1/2,\kappa\delta/(\sqrt2\pi)\}.$$ The same body $K$ then satisfies <a href="#eq:all-diagonal-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:all-diagonal-energy">[eq:all-diagonal-energy]</a> for every positive definite diagonal $Q$. These choices are independent of $d$, $n$ and the vectors.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
###### Choosing the coefficients.

For $u\in\mathbb R$ write $\operatorname{clip}_\delta(u)=\max\{-\delta,\min\{u,\delta\}\}$. Apply Proposition <a href="#prop:adaptive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:adaptive">3</a> to the distinct axes $e_{d+1},\ldots,e_{d+n}$. At step $i$ prescribe the shift $$q_i=\operatorname{clip}_\delta(m_ix^{(i-1)}).$$ By the support of $m_i$, this is a function of the previously assigned coordinates, as required. The proposition supplies signs and a final point $x=x^{(n)}\in K$. Its first $d$ coordinates remain zero. Also, coordinates assigned before step $i$ never change afterward, so $$m_ix^{(i-1)}=m_ix.$$ The final membership $x\in K$ implies $|m_ix|<\delta$. Hence each shift was already within the permitted interval: clipping was inactive, and <a id="eq:unclipped-coordinates"></a>$$\label{eq:unclipped-coordinates}
 x_{d+i}=m_ix+\varepsilon_i
 =\eta_i b_i^TR_{i-1}x+\varepsilon_i.$$ Here $x^0=x$ because the initial block is zero. Clipping is used only to make the application of Proposition <a href="#prop:adaptive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:adaptive">3</a> unconditional; causality and the final constraints subsequently remove it.

Figure <a href="#fig:cancellation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:cancellation">1</a> illustrates the identity that completes the construction.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
###### Cancellation.

<a id="eq:feedback-cancellation"></a>
For this single final coefficient vector, $R_0x=0$ and $$\begin{aligned}
 R_tx
 &=C_tR_{t-1}x+b_tx_{d+t}\\
 &=(I-\eta_tb_tb_t^T)R_{t-1}x
   +b_t(\eta_tb_t^TR_{t-1}x+\varepsilon_t)\\
 &=R_{t-1}x+\varepsilon_tb_t.
\end{aligned}$$ Thus, simultaneously for every $0\le t\le n$, $$\label{eq:feedback-cancellation}
 R_tx=\sum_{i=1}^t\varepsilon_ib_i.$$ Since $x\in2D_*\mathcal D$, all these states have norm less than $2D_*A_0\sqrt d$. Dividing <a href="#eq:feedback-cancellation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:feedback-cancellation">[eq:feedback-cancellation]</a> by $\sigma$ proves Theorem <a href="#thm:signing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signing">1</a> with the absolute constant $C=2D_*A_0/\sigma$. ◻

</details>

<figure id="fig:cancellation">

<figcaption>For the final coefficient vector, put $r = R_{t − 1}x$. The predictor term in the incoming coordinate restores exactly the component removed by $C_{t}$, leaving the signed increment $ε_{t}b_{t}$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-cancellation"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:cancellation`)

TikZ/PGF source, copied verbatim from the source:

```latex
\begin{tikzpicture}[
   >=stealth,
   every node/.style={font=\small},
   state/.style={draw,rounded corners,align=center,minimum height=9mm}]
  \node[state] (r) at (0,0) {$r$};
  \node[state] (contract) at (3.7,0)
    {$C_tr=r-\eta_tb_tb_t^Tr$};
  \node[draw,circle,inner sep=2pt] (sum) at (7.5,0) {$+$};
  \node[state] (out) at (10.5,0) {$r+\varepsilon_tb_t$};
  \node[state] (incoming) at (3.7,-1.5)
    {$b_tx_{d+t}=b_t(\eta_tb_t^Tr+\varepsilon_t)$};
  \draw[->] (r) -- (contract);
  \draw[->] (contract) -- (sum);
  \draw[->] (sum) -- (out);
  \draw[->] (r.south) -- (0,-1.5) -- (incoming.west);
  \draw[->] (incoming.east) -- (7.5,-1.5) -- (sum.south);
 \end{tikzpicture}
```
